# Order 0.6.0 design campaign plan
**Current-state authority:** §7 is the sole owner of current per-concern phase state; §18 is the sole owner of current G0-G5, G5A and G6 gate state. §13A owns only the current `ALIGN-A01`-`ALIGN-A11` / `ALIGN-H1` audit progression state. Header/prose/START-END records are historical or procedural and must not be interpreted as current state.
**WorkItem:** `work_0f6d97bf-5c9e-4ed7-8f4c-2dd8806d4a21`  
**Lifecycle role:** supporting campaign-control and coverage document; **not** R2 authority, technical-design authority, P6 contract authority, or the P7 `implementation-plan.md`  
**Staging branch:** `docs/0.6.0-design-campaign`  
**Foundation baseline:** owned only by the §18 G0 row. Before first governed delivery to `master`, G0 may be `PASS` only when that exact full commit SHA is an ancestor of the active campaign branch `HEAD` and the campaign-state fitness sensor passes. After the campaign package is squash-merged under `GW-DOC-PUBLIC-REPOSITORY-DELIVERY`, the baseline remains exact historical campaign evidence; default-branch descendants must not require the squashed feature-branch commits to remain Git ancestors.
**Governing authorities:** `GW-DOC-WORK-LIFECYCLE`, `GW-DOC-LIFECYCLE-CONTEXT`, `GW-DOC-WORK-ITEM-DOCUMENT-SYSTEM`, `GW-DOC-SPECIFICATION-STANDARD`, `GW-P1-DOC-CANONICALITY`

## 1. Purpose

This document governs the complete Order 0.6.0 design campaign from prerequisite investigation through an adversarially reviewable implementation plan, mandatory vertical functional/technical/implementation alignment closure and final R3 readiness review.

The campaign is intentionally concern-driven rather than screen-driven or implementation-driven. Its primary objective is to reconstruct, from canonical P2/P3 authority, the complete intended functional design that the 0.5.0 work was supposed to realize; then derive the 0.6.0 technical solution design and implementation plan that will actually realize that design. The released 0.5.0 application, its tests and its rendered UI are evidence only and never define what 0.6.0 must preserve. The campaign makes omissions visible by ensuring that every material product concern has an explicit investigation start, an explicit factual end, a functional-design disposition, a technical-design disposition and final implementation-plan coverage.

The following is the **sole normative campaign progression for this WorkItem**. No other WorkItem artifact, modular PLAN source, readiness artifact, PR body, handover, prompt or summary may restate, shorten, extend or independently maintain a competing campaign progression:

```text
G0 — Phase 0 factual/governance baseline established
  |
  v
PHASE 1 / R1
complete concern inventory + evidence-first concern investigations
  |
  | G1/G2: coverage proven; every investigation factually closed
  v
PHASE 2A / R2
complete 0.6.0 functional requirements / functional design
  |
  | G3: all concerns functionally closed + horizontal integration PASS
  v
PHASE 2B / DESIGN
complete technical solution design / P4-P6 consequences
  |
  | G4: all functional obligations technically realized
  v
PHASE 3 / PLAN
final executable implementation plan
  |
  | G5: complete P1->P7 traceability, no orphan task or uncovered requirement
  v
PHASE 3A / ALIGN
final vertical domain alignment across R2 -> DESIGN -> PLAN -> proof
  |
  | G5A: A01-A11 + ALIGN-H1 complete, semantically aligned and executable
  v
PHASE 4 / R3
adversarial readiness review of the exact current P1-P7 package
  |
  | G6: READY_FOR_SIGNOFF
  v
BOUNDED FOUNDATION REALIZATION / EVIDENCE
when §17A is required: explicit exact-package operator grant -> IA27 foundation -> IA28 first-consumer acceptance
  |
  | evidence reviewed; bounded grant terminates; stop before IA29
  v
explicit sign-off / normal implementation authority
```

Normal signed implementation is outside this campaign plan and begins only after the canonical PLAN and R3 package has been explicitly signed off. The sole pre-sign-off exception is the bounded IA27/IA28 acceptance-evidence realization in §17A. Consistent with `GW-DOC-SYSTEMS-GOVERNANCE-PYRAMID-V1`, that tranche is eligible only after fresh R3/G6 `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`; a fresh exact-package operator authorization may then permit only IA27 foundation reconstruction and IA28 first-consumer acceptance, must terminate before IA29, and exists only to supply evidence for the subsequent human evidence-acceptance/sign-off decision.

## 2. Canonical 0.6.0 work point

This WorkItem is the **single active campaign/control point for Order 0.6.0 design work**:

`work_0f6d97bf-5c9e-4ed7-8f4c-2dd8806d4a21`

All 0.6.0 prerequisite investigation, concern coverage, functional-design status, technical-design status, implementation-plan coverage and readiness status are tracked here or in this WorkItem's canonical lifecycle artifacts.

This designation is a campaign/work coordination rule, not a new semantic authority layer:

- permanent P1-P6 rules remain owned by their canonical `GW-DOC-*` / requirement / technical-requirement owners;
- `research.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-design.md`, `implementation-plan.md` and R3 remain the lifecycle-owned package artifacts;
- findings discovered elsewhere that affect the 0.6.0 campaign must be linked back to this WorkItem rather than spawning an untracked parallel 0.6.0 design stream;
- a separate WorkItem is created only where the lifecycle requires independently governed remediation or unrelated work; its relationship back to this campaign must be recorded here;
- no other plan, handover, prompt, review or branch may act as a competing 0.6.0 campaign status owner.

The current documentation index points to this WorkItem as the active 0.6.0 campaign. Git history/PRs remain delivery provenance; this WorkItem remains the design-campaign tracking point until the campaign reaches G6 and explicit sign-off.

### 2.1 Single-plan authority and precedence

For this WorkItem, `0.6.0-design-campaign-plan.md` is the **sole campaign-process authority**. It determines:

- investigation ordering;
- two-concern batch definitions;
- mandatory pair-audit procedure and current pair-audit status;
- concern START/END control;
- finding/dependency routing;
- per-concern campaign phase state;
- G0-G5, G5A and G6 gate state;
- horizontal and final vertical alignment audit requirements;
- progression permission between R1, R2, DESIGN, PLAN, ALIGN and R3.

There is no second campaign plan.

The one canonical P7 artifact is `implementation-plan.md`, which remains the deterministic lifecycle artifact, sole normative execution authority and sole `order-tasks` carrier. PLAN may be authored through modular phase source files under `implementation-plan/`, but their complete phase/action/verification content must be compiled into the root and the root must list every source component by exact Git blob SHA. The directory files own no independent lifecycle status or task set. Executable task authority exists only when the compiled root and its source manifest are current and G5 can legitimately close.

Document roles are closed:

| Document | Exclusive role | Must not be used as |
|---|---|---|
| `0.6.0-design-campaign-plan.md` | campaign process, pair audits, findings/dependencies, status/gates/progression | product specification, technical architecture, P6 contract, P7 task set |
| `0.6.0-concern-coverage-ledger.md` | concern/authority inventory evidence | semantic completeness proof, status owner, implementation plan |
| `research.md` | current R1 factual synthesis | campaign status owner, R2 requirement source beyond its facts, implementation plan |
| `requirements.md` | current R2 functional requirements | campaign status owner, technical design, P7 task plan |
| `solution-design.md` | current DESIGN technical solution | campaign status owner, P7 task plan |
| `implementation-plan.md` | sole normative P7 task/change/verification artifact after PLAN closure; may contain a compiled projection of exact-hash-bound `implementation-plan/` authoring sources | campaign sequencing/audit authority, P1-P6 semantic authority, independent directory execution authority |
| `readiness-review.md` | current R3 adversarial verdict/return | repair document, campaign status owner outside its verdict consequence |

No additional competing plan may be created inside this WorkItem to own prerequisite completion, remediation, audit cadence, implementation scope or campaign status. The §1 campaign progression is stated once as the overall normative sequence; later sections define the rules of their named stage/gate and may reference §1, but must not create or maintain a second end-to-end progression. Sibling lifecycle artifacts and delivery/provenance surfaces may identify their own bounded role or verdict only; they must reference this plan for campaign sequencing/readiness. `implementation-plan/` phase files are permitted only as modular authoring sources whose complete content is compiled into the canonical root PLAN; they must not carry independent lifecycle status, execution authority or another `order-tasks` block.

### 2.2 Precedence against stale or external information

The following can provide evidence or provenance but can never independently advance, reopen, close or redefine the campaign:

- historical concern START/END records;
- stale R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN/R3 content retained for reuse;
- prior release behavior;
- current implementation/UI/tests;
- CI or PR status;
- PR descriptions/review comments;
- handovers or session notes;
- prompts;
- model/chat output;
- documentation from another WorkItem.

When such material conflicts with the current authoritative role owner:

1. it is classified as evidence of a possible defect, stale assumption or reusable prior work;
2. the owning canonical document is re-opened if the evidence is material;
3. the owner is amended or revalidated through the governed lifecycle;
4. the lower-authority material never overrides the owner merely because it is newer, detailed or executable.

Progression permission is derived only from the current-state owners declared in this document: §7 for per-concern state, §8.4 for phase-local pair-audit progression, §13A for ALIGN substage progression, and §18 for G0-G5/G5A/G6 gate state. The sole overall campaign progression is the §1 sequence. No prose elsewhere may synthesize a competing current state or maintain a second overall progression.

## 3. Relationship to the Order lifecycle

This WorkItem uses `change-v2`.

The lifecycle artifacts retain their canonical meanings:

| Lifecycle stage | Canonical artifact | Campaign meaning |

| R1 | `research.md` | final factual synthesis from all concern investigations |
| R2 | `requirements.md` | binding 0.6.0 functional requirements / functional design outcomes |

| PLAN | `implementation-plan.md` | executable P7 implementation task set and verification plan |
| R3 | `readiness-review.md` | adversarial challenge of the entire current package; no separate human/worker is assumed |

This campaign document does not replace any of those artifacts. It owns only:

- campaign sequencing;
- concern coverage;
- concern investigation start/end control;
- finding and cross-concern dependency tracking;
- phase/gate status;
- evidence that all required concerns were processed.

Any normative product behavior discovered or selected during the campaign must be written to the correct canonical owner/lifecycle artifact rather than stated here as a parallel specification.

## 4. No current-state fallback / no regression-baseline rule

The 0.6.0 campaign is an authority-driven design campaign. **Current application state, current UI composition, current implementation behavior and current design shape are not campaign outputs and are never a fallback definition of desired behavior.**

Current code, tests, rendered UI, historical design documents and prior releases may be inspected only as transient evidence to:

- prove a defect or contradiction against canonical authority;
- identify an existing capability that a later DESIGN decision may deliberately reuse;
- locate an ownership/boundary failure;
- determine migration/deletion consequences after the required future behavior is already established;
- prove that a claimed correction actually landed.

They must **not** be copied into this plan, `research.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-design.md` or another campaign artifact as a descriptive inventory of "how the application currently works", a preservation baseline, compatibility target, regression contract, fallback behavior or implicit requirement.

The governing direction is always:

```text
canonical product intent / authority
        -> factual gap or unresolved decision
        -> required 0.6.0 behavior
        -> technical realization
        -> implementation plan
```

and never:

```text
current application/design state
        -> preserve by default
        -> document as desired behavior
```

A concern investigation therefore records only:

1. applicable canonical authority and accepted intent;
2. contradictions, missing decisions, ownership defects or unproven obligations;
3. exact decision inputs required by R2/DESIGN;
4. cross-concern dependencies;
5. verification evidence sufficient to support the finding.

Implementation observations remain cited evidence behind a finding; they are not restated as product facts unless the same statement is independently owned by canonical authority. Absence of a finding does not mean "keep the current application"; it means the existing canonical authority is sufficient for that concern.

Any wording such as "preserve current", "keep existing", "match current", "unchanged behavior", "current design is correct", or equivalent is invalid unless it refers to an explicitly cited canonical semantic obligation rather than incidental implementation/design state.

### 4.1 Campaign-purpose precedence

The purpose of this campaign outranks transient evidence and delivery status. A PR/CI result, browser test, existing regression, current implementation behavior, historical design statement, concern status note or handover may not become a functional requirement, preservation contract or campaign gate by implication. Such evidence may stop the campaign only when it proves that the canonical authority, document-authoring contract or conformance machinery needed to reconstruct R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN is itself defective. Otherwise it is routed to the appropriate later design, implementation or delivery work and the authority-driven reconstruction continues.

Browser/UI tests become relevant to 0.6.0 acceptance only after reconstructed functional requirements and technical design establish the behavior they must verify. Existing browser tests do not constrain the reconstructed design merely because they describe or protect 0.5.0 behavior.

Direct operator findings from rendered 0.6.0 review are durable acceptance evidence, not transient chat commentary. The canonical implementation plan action catalog owns the binding `HUX-*` human-acceptance ledger. A machine/test/rendered-evidence PASS cannot close those rows; affected implementation phases and final IA26/IA24 progression remain blocked until fresh corrected evidence is explicitly accepted by the operator. This ledger does not create a second concern-status owner; it is the P7/acceptance disposition of concrete rendered defects.

### 4.2 Exhaustive semantic and realization proof

The prior campaign pass established broad authority/concern coverage but did not prove the complete semantic content and realization of every applicable obligation. That failure invalidates the former G2-G6 closure and requires R1 revalidation before downstream lifecycle artifacts can be trusted again.

The campaign must distinguish three independent completeness proofs:

1. **Authority coverage** — every applicable canonical owner, stable requirement and normative section has been examined.
2. **Semantic/information coverage** — every material obligation has been atomized into its required information, hierarchy, work-kind/stage variation, interaction, state, transition, failure, recovery, negative behavior and acceptance consequence.
3. **Realization coverage** — every obligation has a complete producer -> carrier -> consumer path through the actual authoring/schema/prompt/state/contract/runtime/browser/presentation boundaries that apply to it.

For every material obligation, current realization evidence is classified only as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`. `UNPROVEN` prevents factual closure.

Concern mapping and stable-ID mapping are necessary inventory controls only. They are not semantic-conformance proof. Likewise, `REFERENCE`, `no functional delta`, `no technical change`, `no implementation change` and `already satisfied` are evidence-backed verdicts, never defaults.

Where the canonical authority defines screen- or stage-specific information design, the audit must be screen/stage specific. At minimum the Workbench proof covers every applicable combination of workflow step, work kind, interaction condition, current-attention condition, material/currentness condition, current/historical selection and responsive class. The audit must prove the information producer, canonical carrier, presentation derivation, rendered hierarchy and valid action for each material value.

### 4.3 G-F12 — final functional/solution-design reconstruction audit

**Finding:** `G-F12` — the campaign already required exhaustive work-kind/stage/information/realization analysis, but the downstream closure criteria still permitted that semantic content to collapse into broad `REFERENCE`, `POSITIVE_NO_CHANGE`, concern-summary or verification-only rows. As a result, a pair/ALIGN audit could declare Q2/Q3/Q12 complete while the current `requirements.md` and `solution-design.md` did not themselves carry the full applicable functional and technical description needed to implement the intended 0.6.0 system without reconstructing semantics from implementation, tests or scattered evidence.

This is a **campaign-process completeness defect**, not new product authority. It changes how completeness is proven; it does not create or select product behavior. Canonical P2/P3/P4-P6 owners remain the semantic authorities.

The remediation is one final design-set reconstruction audit across **all C01-C22**, using the existing A01-A11 dependency order. Previous content may be reused only after the exact obligation passes the strengthened checks below. Previous PASS/closed/no-change verdicts are historical evidence and are not inherited.

For every applicable combination of:

`concern × work kind × workflow step × information item × interaction condition × current-attention condition × material/currentness condition × current/historical selection × responsive class`

the final audit must establish, as applicable:

1. canonical semantic authority and the exact required information/behavior;
2. the operator/agent information that is displayed, entered, selected, generated or reviewed;
3. primary/supporting/technical hierarchy and the valid current action;
4. state, transition, blocker, failure, recovery, return and terminal behavior;
5. authoring schema/template and lifecycle applicability/terminalization;
6. OpenCode/STAGE context, prompt/template specialization, provider role/permission/session behavior and required-input/proposal consequences;
7. governed output/document/event/state carrier;
8. pure policy/derivation and daemon/shell/persistence/transport boundaries;
9. Workbench interaction class, renderer/primitive composition and responsive/focus/theme consequence;
10. downstream consumer, next-stage input and verification proposition.

The three lifecycle artifacts have different mandatory responsibilities:

- **R1 / `research.md`** must contain the complete factual/realization analysis needed to determine whether the canonical obligation is currently realized, violated or unproven. Where an area is incomplete, that analysis is redone from authority and evidence; implementation/UI/tests remain evidence only.
- **R2 / `requirements.md`** must contain the complete applicable **functional description**, even for `REFERENCE` obligations: required information, hierarchy, interaction, work-kind/stage variation, state/action/transition, failure/recovery/negative behavior, acceptance, scope/exclusions and downstream functional consequence. A reference identifies the canonical owner; it does not substitute for the explicit 0.6.0 functional consequence.
- **DESIGN / `solution-design.md`** must contain the complete applicable **technical realization**, even for no-change/reuse obligations: canonical owner, shared-versus-kind/stage-specific boundary, producer→carrier→consumer/data-control path, schema/template/context/prompt/provider consequences, state/event/document carrier, pure/effect ownership, UI composition, failure/recovery, reuse/removal/replacement decision, downstream invariants and exact permanent proof owner. Current implementation may positively evidence that realization but may never supply missing design semantics by implication.

A functional or technical row may cite a permanent canonical owner rather than duplicate its normative text, but the lifecycle artifact must state the complete package-specific consequence and realization needed for zero-inference execution. This is not permission to create a shadow owner.

**No certification-only substitution:** a browser/integration/certification action may prove a fully specified functional/technical contract; it may not stand in for one. In particular, scenario matrices such as work-kind certification are downstream proof of the explicit R2/DESIGN model, not the design itself.

**No generic shared-machinery substitution:** shared lifecycle, provider, document, dialogue, component or state machinery must be described once at its canonical integration owner, but every work-kind/stage consumer must explicitly map to that machinery and state its specialization/no-specialization consequence. "Uses existing shared flow" is insufficient without the mapping.

**Re-entry consequence:** activation of `G-F12` requires the campaign to invalidate semantic closure from G2 onward; the criterion change itself does not invalidate the G0/G1 foundation/inventory unless the audit discovers a new foundation or inventory defect. R1 pair audits must be freshly rerun first; any deficient R1 area is corrected in `research.md`. R2 and DESIGN are then reconstructed/revalidated against the strengthened standard before PLAN, ALIGN and R3 may be regenerated/revalidated. The ordinary sequence remains G2 → G3 → G4 → G5 → G5A → G6. Current gate state is read only from §18.

The existing campaign material is recovered selectively:
- canonical P1-P6 authority remains authoritative unless the audit proves a defect in that owner;
- G-F01 through G-F05 remain reusable foundation corrections subject to freshness revalidation;
- prior concern authority facts, evidence references and implementation observations remain reusable evidence;
- prior concern closure verdicts, grouped no-delta conclusions, exact DESIGN no-change conclusions, the ten-path T1 scope and the former R3 readiness verdict are not current premises and must be independently re-proven;
- all normative replacement content is written into the existing canonical lifecycle artifacts or permanent canonical owners. No parallel functional/design/plan documentation set is permitted.

## 5. Phase 0 entry gate — G0

G0 is the factual-baseline gate. It passes when all of the following are true:

- one exact Phase 0 candidate commit is recorded as the campaign baseline;
- the campaign branch contains that candidate's authority/governance state;
- the six Phase 0 advanced concerns resolve through stable IDs to their current owners and retain their historical V1 semantic sources;
- the current documentation-topology/architecture sensor passes;
- the authority/topology/document-authoring/conformance checks required to trust R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN production are green on the exact candidate; a failing build/browser/release/delivery check does not block G0 unless that failure itself proves one of those campaign-foundation mechanisms is defective;
- there is no unresolved foundational authority ambiguity known at campaign start.

PR merge state is recorded as delivery provenance but does not block fact gathering or document completion. There is no independent reviewer/second worker in this operating environment, so G0 must not depend on a fabricated third-party approval.

Phase 0 remains subject to downstream validation. If functional-design work exposes a foundational authority defect, the campaign stops at the affected concern, records the evidence, repairs the canonical foundation through the appropriate governed WorkItem, and then revalidates the affected concern before proceeding.

### 5.1 Campaign current-state ownership and mutation law

Current campaign state is deliberately single-owned and mechanically constrained.

- **§7 Master concern register is the only current owner of C01-C22 phase/overall state.**
- **§18 Phase/gate status register is the only current owner of G0-G5/G5A/G6 state and the exact G0 foundation-baseline SHA.**
- document headers, narrative summaries, START/END records, historical checkpoint prose and handovers may record evidence or history but must not declare or override current campaign state;
- when G2 is `IN_PROGRESS`, the active R1 concern is derived from §7 rather than separately named elsewhere;
- a gate may not advance unless every preceding gate required by the campaign progression is `PASS`;
- before first governed delivery of this campaign package to `master`, G0 may be `PASS` only if its full baseline SHA is an ancestor of the active campaign branch `HEAD`, no `OPEN_FOUNDATION` finding remains, and the campaign-state fitness sensor passes;
- after the campaign package has been integrated into `master` through the canonical squash-merge delivery path, default-branch descendants preserve the exact G0 baseline as historical evidence and prove integration from the default-branch campaign package rather than requiring squashed feature-branch commits to remain ancestors;
- a downstream foundational defect changes the affected gate/register state first, is remediated through the governed owner/WorkItem, and is integrated into campaign history by ancestry before that gate can return to `PASS` while the campaign is still pre-delivery;
- before first delivery, copying equivalent files, citing another branch, recording a PR result or relying on prior-session prose is not evidence of branch containment;
- after any campaign-state mutation, run the permanent campaign-state fitness sensor and derive the reported state again from §7/§18.

This is a campaign-control rule owned here. It does not create a second product, lifecycle, architecture, contract or execution-state model.

## 6. Coverage model

The campaign uses two independent completeness proofs.

### 6.1 Concern coverage

Every material product/design concern must appear in the concern register in §7.

The concern register is derived from current P2/P3 authority, not from the current UI navigation alone. Before C01 begins, Phase 1 must prove that the register covers all current stable functional requirement families and all user-visible workflow surfaces.

If a missing concern is discovered later, it is added to the register with a new stable `Cxx` identifier before analysis continues.

### 6.2 Requirement-to-concern coverage

Phase 1 must establish a coverage ledger:

```text
stable P2/P3 requirement / concern
    -> one or more Cxx concerns
    -> investigation evidence
    -> R2 disposition
    -> DESIGN realization
    -> PLAN task coverage
```

No P3 requirement may remain unmapped. A concern may cover multiple requirements; a requirement may legitimately span concerns, but one concern must be marked primary owner for campaign tracking.

## 8. Concern investigation protocol

Every concern uses the same explicit START -> investigation -> END protocol.

### 8.1 START record

Before evidence collection for a concern begins, add a START record to the concern log in this document:

```text
CONCERN START: Cxx — <name>

Start baseline:
- exact repository commit:
- date:
- current phase: R1

Canonical owners:
- ...

Requirement families:
- ...

Upstream intent:
- ...

Known downstream consumers:
- ...

Incoming cross-concern findings:
- ...

In scope:
- ...

Explicitly out of scope:
- ...

Questions to close:
1. What does canonical authority require for this concern?
2. Is that authority complete and internally consistent for 0.6.0?
3. What exact gaps or decisions remain before future behavior is unambiguous?
4. Which adjacent concerns can invalidate this concern's closure?
```

A concern is then changed from `NOT_STARTED` to `IN_REVIEW`.

### 8.2 Mandatory R1 analysis dimensions

Each concern investigation must close:

1. **Intent** — canonical P2/product reason and required user outcome.
2. **Authority** — canonical owner(s), stable requirement identities and applicable higher rules.
3. **Authority completeness** — contradictions, missing decisions, ambiguous ownership or unproven obligations that prevent a complete 0.6.0 decision.
4. **Information ownership** — required canonical source facts and semantic producer -> carrier -> consumer ownership where the authority depends on them.
5. **Interaction obligations** — only the operator-visible behavior required or left undecided by canonical authority.
6. **State/transition closure** — required lifecycle/material/proposal/approval/task/provider semantics and any missing decision.
7. **Negative behavior** — prohibited behavior and failure semantics owned by authority.
8. **Recovery obligations** — blocker, retry, stale, cancellation and return-to-owner requirements.
9. **Cross-concern interfaces** — authority/decision dependencies imported from or exported to other Cxx concerns.
10. **Proof state** — obligation-level evidence sufficient to classify every material current realization as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`, without turning observed implementation/design state into a campaign-owned baseline.
12. **Authoring/input/output path** — schema/template/prompt/context inputs, generated outputs and structural/semantic conformance needed to preserve the obligation precisely.
13. **Realization path** — complete producer -> carrier -> consumer flow through canonical contracts/state, core/daemon/shell, persistence/transport, browser projection and rendered/operator consumption where applicable.
14. **Information design** — exact primary, secondary and technical/on-demand information, including work-kind/stage distinctions and current-attention overrides wherever user-visible.
15. **Verification ownership** — the permanent sensor or semantic review needed to prove the obligation rather than merely the existence of its identifier.

Current code, tests, UI and prior design may be inspected to prove a finding, but their incidental shape is never recorded as desired behavior, preservation scope or fallback semantics.

### 8.3 END record

A concern investigation cannot stop informally. It ends with:

```text
CONCERN END: Cxx — <name>

End baseline:
- exact repository commit:
- date:

R1 verdict:
VERIFIED | GAP_FOUND | BLOCKED

Authority facts / decision inputs established:
- ...

Findings:
- Cxx-F01 ...
- ...

Cross-concern findings emitted:
- X-Cxx-Cyy-01 ...
- ...

Incoming findings resolved:
- ...

Canonical owners implicated:
- ...

Open product decisions for R2:
- NONE | ...

Open factual questions:
- NONE | ...

R1 disposition:
FACTS_CLOSED | GAP_FOUND | BLOCKED

Revalidation trigger:
- ...
```

`VERIFIED` means the factual model is closed; it does **not** mean no 0.6.0 functional change is needed.

No concern reaches R2 while it has an unresolved factual question that could materially change the functional decision.

### 8.4 Mandatory two-concern audit cadence

The campaign is executed in bounded two-concern batches. Completing two concerns at a lifecycle phase does **not** authorize work on a third concern at that phase. The exact prior two completed concerns must first be audited against the full detailed requirements for that phase.

This cadence applies independently to R1, R2, DESIGN and PLAN.

The pair sequence follows the dependency order in §10.2:

| Pair | Concerns |
|---|---|
| A01 | C01 + C21 |
| A02 | C22 + C02 |
| A03 | C03 + C04 |
| A04 | C05 + C06 |
| A05 | C07 + C08 |
| A06 | C09 + C10 |
| A07 | C11 + C12 |
| A08 | C13 + C14 |
| A09 | C15 + C16 |
| A10 | C17 + C18 |
| A11 | C19 + C20 |

A pair may be reordered only when a newly proven dependency requires it. The campaign plan must record the dependency evidence and amend the pair sequence **before** work begins on the affected pair.

#### Pair-audit progression law

For a given phase:

1. the two concerns in the current pair are completed under the normal phase rules;
2. the pair audit enters `IN_REVIEW`;
3. the auditor reopens the exact current artifacts/evidence for both concerns and performs every applicable general and phase-specific check below;
4. the audit returns `PASS` or `FAIL`;
5. `PASS` authorizes the next pair at that phase;
6. `FAIL` reopens the defective concern(s) at the exact owning phase and the same pair must be re-audited after correction;
7. a missing audit is progression-equivalent to `FAIL`.

No model, handover, status summary or later lifecycle artifact may claim progress in pair A(n+1) while pair An lacks a current `PASS` at that same phase.

The audit must be adversarial. It may reuse prior evidence, but it must not trust the two concerns' own completion assertions.

#### General pair-audit checklist

Every pair audit must prove, for **each** of its two concerns:

1. all applicable canonical owners and normative clauses were actually inspected;
2. every material obligation was atomized rather than hidden behind a requirement ID, concern ID or broad summary;
3. every mandatory phase dimension was answered with evidence;
4. evidence is current for the exact reviewed repository/document baseline;
5. implementation/UI/tests/history were used only as evidence and never as desired-state authority;
6. every producer -> carrier -> consumer interface required by the concern is accounted for;
7. every applicable state, transition, interaction, failure, recovery and negative behavior is explicit;
8. work-kind and workflow-stage variants are explicit where applicable;
9. every cross-concern dependency/finding has an owner and is not silently deferred;
10. reused prior material is classified and independently justified rather than inherited by status;
11. there is no duplicate/shadow canonical owner created by the concern artifact;
12. no material item is left `UNPROVEN`.

The audit also challenges the **interface between the two concerns**: assumptions exported by one must agree with facts/requirements/owners consumed by the other.

#### R1 pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, R1 pair audit must prove:

- complete authority coverage beneath stable IDs, including normative prose without a dedicated ID;
- complete semantic/information coverage;
- complete realization coverage;
- exact schema/template/prompt/context input and generated-output obligations where agentic authoring applies;
- exact canonical carrier/state/event/document ownership;
- exact core/daemon/shell/persistence/transport/browser/presentation path where applicable;
- Workbench primary/secondary/technical information hierarchy where user-visible;
- applicable concern × workflow-step × work-kind × information-item × interaction × current-attention × material/currentness × current/historical-selection × responsive-class consequences, including schema/template/context/prompt/provider and UI producer→carrier→consumer paths where applicable;
- explicit identification of any area whose current R1 synthesis is too compressed to support a complete R2/DESIGN description; such an area is redone in R1 rather than silently reconstructed downstream;
- current realization classified only as `CONFORMS`, `VIOLATES` or `UNPROVEN`;
- zero material `UNPROVEN` items before `PASS`.

An R1 audit must fail if a concern merely proves what authority says but does not prove how the applicable obligation is currently realized or violated.

#### R2 pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, R2 pair audit must prove:

- every material R1 obligation has an explicit functional consequence;
- each material P3 obligation has exactly one valid `REFERENCE | DELTA | LOCAL` relationship where the v2 authority-hygiene schema requires it;
- `REFERENCE` preserves canonical ownership but does not replace explicit required 0.6.0 behavior;
- for every applicable work-kind/stage/information combination, the R2 artifact itself states the complete package-specific functional consequence: displayed/entered/generated/reviewed information, primary/supporting/technical hierarchy, interaction, state/action/transition, blocker/failure/recovery/return/terminal behavior, acceptance, scope/exclusions and downstream consequence;
- work-kind/stage distinctions from R1 remain preserved through every applicable shared delivery step; a generic concern summary or certification row cannot substitute for those distinctions;
- no implementation choice has leaked into P3 unless that choice is itself binding authority;
- no grouped `no functional delta` statement hides multiple unexamined obligations;
- zero unresolved product/authority decisions remain for the pair.

#### DESIGN pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, DESIGN pair audit must prove:

- every R2 obligation has an explicit technical realization;
- canonical semantic owner and affected boundaries are identified;
- pure/core derivation versus daemon/shell/UI effect ownership is explicit;
- complete data/control and producer -> carrier -> consumer paths are specified for every applicable functional combination;
- schema/template/catalog/context/prompt/provider/session/permission/document/event/state/UI-composition consequences are explicit where applicable, including the shared-versus-kind/stage-specific boundary and explicit `no change` proof where applicable;
- every R2 work-kind/stage/information contract has a named technical carrier and downstream consumer; generic "existing/shared flow" or verification-only dispositions are insufficient without that mapping;
- existing capability to reuse and incorrect behavior/owner to remove or replace are explicit;
- necessary new concepts are justified and no DTO/service/repository/controller/shadow-model family is introduced;
- FCIS, SoC, DRY/canonical ownership, strict typing and minimum-change constraints are satisfied;
- failure/recovery and permanent verification ownership are explicit;
- `No technical change` is accepted only with positive evidence that existing P4-P6 realization and current implementation satisfy the complete obligation;
- zero material architecture choices are delegated to implementation.

#### PLAN pair-audit requirements

In addition to the general checklist, PLAN pair audit must prove:

- every DESIGN obligation maps downward to at least one exact task action or an evidence-backed no-change/no-code disposition;
- every planned action maps upward to accepted requirement/design authority;
- expected production, test/validation and documentation/contract surfaces are explicit;
- required deletions/replacements are explicit;
- dependencies and ordering are executable;
- architecture-feedback checkpoints are present where required;
- verification gates prove required behavior, preserved required behavior, architecture/governance, authorized scope and minimum sufficiency;
- task/change surfaces do not force duplicate responsibility or hide an unsigned architecture decision;
- no implementation worker must choose a material P1-P6 decision;
- exact task scope is not narrowed by an unsupported earlier `no change` conclusion.

#### Pair-audit record format

Every audit is recorded in this campaign plan using:

```text
PAIR AUDIT START: <R1|R2|DESIGN|PLAN>-Axx

Concerns:
- Cxx
- Cyy

Exact baseline:
- repository commit:
- artifact hashes/SHAs:

Prerequisite:
- prior pair audit: PASS | N/A for A01

Detailed checks:
- general checklist: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- phase-specific checklist: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- cross-concern interface: PASS | FAIL with evidence
- reused-material revalidation: PASS | FAIL with evidence

Findings:
- ...

Reopened concerns:
- NONE | ...

Unproven items:
- NONE | ...

PAIR AUDIT END: <R1|R2|DESIGN|PLAN>-Axx
Verdict: PASS | FAIL
Next pair authorized: YES | NO
```

A `PASS` without evidence against the detailed checks is invalid.

## 9. Finding and dependency discipline

### 9.1 Stable finding identities

Use:

- `Cxx-Fnn` — finding owned by one concern;
- `X-Cxx-Cyy-nn` — finding discovered in Cxx but requiring disposition in Cyy;
- `G-Fnn` — genuinely campaign-wide foundational finding;
- `ALIGN-Axx-Fnn` — finding discovered by a vertical ALIGN audit. This is a campaign-local trace identity only: the finding must be routed to the earliest lifecycle/canonical owner that can correct it and must never become a new semantic authority owner.

A finding is recorded once at its primary owner and referenced elsewhere. ALIGN findings remain open until every required upstream/foundation/PLAN correction is complete and the affected ALIGN audit is freshly rerun; correcting an upstream document alone does not resolve an ALIGN finding whose executable baseline or downstream package is still stale.

For current A06 findings, `OPEN_FOUNDATION` means the governing authority correction is documented but the required execution-baseline capability has not yet been implemented, integrated by ancestry and independently verified; it therefore keeps G0 in revalidation. `OPEN_PLAN` means no further upstream/foundation semantic correction is required, but the final regenerated PLAN still must incorporate the owned correction before the finding can resolve.

### 9.2 Cross-concern rule

When C06 exposes a problem in C11, C06 records `X-C06-C11-nn`; C06 does not redesign Approval while still investigating Research.

The C11 START record must list that incoming finding. C11 cannot close until it has explicitly disposed it.

### 9.3 Foundational defect rule

If an investigation exposes a Phase 0-class problem—ambiguous canonical ownership, broken reference lineage, shadow authority, impossible lifecycle topology, or another foundation defect—the active concern becomes `BLOCKED`.

The defect is remediated at its canonical owner through a separately governed WorkItem. After that remediation lands, all concerns whose evidence/authority depended on the changed foundation enter `REVALIDATION`.

## 10. Phase 1 — R1 prerequisite investigation

### 10.1 Phase 1 objectives

Phase 1 must produce:

- the proven complete concern register;
- P2/P3 requirement-to-concern coverage ledger;
- one START/END record per concern;
- concern-local findings;
- cross-concern finding ledger;
- evidence needed to support authority gaps and later design decisions, without recording application/design state as a baseline;
- a final `research.md` synthesis.

Phase 1 does not choose final UI composition, new product behavior, technical architecture, APIs, persistence models or implementation abstractions.

### 10.2 Investigation order

Use dependency order rather than visual navigation order:

```text
C01 lifecycle/product model
  -> C21 product identity/language/claims
  -> C22 project governance/authority integration
  -> C02/C03/C04 WorkItem/source/queue foundations
  -> C05 workspace/navigation
  -> C06/C07/C08/C09/C10 pre-signoff stages
  -> C11 Approval
  -> C12 Execution
  -> C13 Complete
  -> C14 shared materials
  -> C15 provider interaction
  -> C16 settings/readiness
  -> C17 recovery/current attention
  -> C18 responsive/accessibility
  -> C19 component/theme consistency
  -> C20 horizontal certification/coherence
```

This order may be adjusted only when an explicit dependency discovered in the evidence requires it. The change and reason are recorded here.

### 10.3 G1 — inventory gate

G1 passes before C01 substantive analysis when:

- every canonical P2/P3 requirement family maps to at least one Cxx;
- every governed user-visible surface/flow maps to at least one Cxx;
- every lifecycle stage maps to at least one Cxx;
- every governed productive provider/settings boundary maps to at least one Cxx;
- there is no known material concern outside the register.

### 10.4 G2 — investigation gate

G2 passes only when:

- every concern has an END record;
- every concern R1 status is `FACTS_CLOSED` or `GAP_FOUND`, never `IN_REVIEW`/`BLOCKED`;
- R1 pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- every cross-concern finding has an owning destination;
- no material factual question remains open;
- `research.md` synthesizes the concern evidence without duplicating permanent authority;
- R2 can begin from closed authority facts and decision inputs without reconstructing an application-state inventory.

## 11. Phase 2A — R2 functional requirements / functional design

### 11.1 Per-concern functional closure

R2 processes the same C01-C22 register.

For each concern, R2 records:

- current authority disposition: `REFERENCE | DELTA | LOCAL` as required by `change-v2`;
- required 0.6.0 user-visible/observable behavior;
- information hierarchy and interaction obligations where applicable;
- state/action/transition/failure/recovery behavior;
- explicit exclusions and non-goals;
- acceptance criteria;
- dependencies on other concerns;
- any permanent P2/P3 owner that must be amended.

A concern reaches `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED` only when an implementer/technical designer would not need to invent product behavior for that concern and every applicable semantic/information obligation has an explicit 0.6.0 consequence. A `REFERENCE` disposition preserves the existing canonical owner; it does not permit the package to collapse that owner's required behavior into a generic "no functional delta" statement.

### 11.2 Functional-design ownership

Normative functional decisions belong in `requirements.md` and the appropriate permanent P2/P3 canonical owner.

This campaign plan records status and references only. It must not become the only place where a product rule exists.

### 11.3 G3 — functional-design gate

G3 requires:

- C01-C22 all `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED`;
- R2 pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- all R1 findings disposed;
- all cross-concern functional dependencies resolved;
- one complete end-to-end user-flow walkthrough from creation through terminal result;
- the final functional design explicitly covers every applicable work-kind × workflow-step × information/interaction combination under §4.3, including Change/Defect downstream divergence and Investigation/Audit terminal paths;
- no functional obligation is represented only by a stable-ID/REFERENCE row, concern summary or certification requirement without its complete package-specific behavior;
- all return/recovery paths included;
- complete P2 -> P3 coverage;
- no contradictory current-attention, lifecycle, material, approval, provider or settings rule;
- a horizontal functional integration audit returns PASS.

If G3 fails, affected concerns return to R2 or R1 as appropriate.

## 12. Phase 2B — DESIGN technical solution design

### 12.1 Per-concern technical closure

DESIGN consumes only functionally closed concerns and determines, as applicable:

- canonical semantic owner;
- pure/core derivation vs daemon/shell/UI effect ownership;
- data/control flow;
- contract and schema consequences;
- persistence/transport consequences;
- provider integration consequences;
- existing capabilities to reuse;
- behavior/owners to delete or replace;
- minimum architecture-preserving decomposition;
- failure/rollback behavior;
- permanent verification sensor ownership.

The design must preserve FCIS, SoC, DRY/canonical ownership, strict typing, no-OOP/no-DTO posture and minimum sufficient change.

A concern reaches `TECHNICAL_CLOSED` only when no material architectural choice is delegated to implementation and every functional obligation has a proven technical realization. `No technical change` is permitted only when DESIGN proves the existing canonical owner, complete producer/carrier/consumer path and current realization already satisfy the obligation.

### 12.2 Authority amendments

Any P4/P5/P6 change is made at its existing canonical owner. The design records stable IDs and exact semantic-version pins where the package requires a fixed snapshot.

No concern-local duplicate contract/model is permitted for convenience.

### 12.3 G4 — technical-design gate

G4 requires:

- every functional requirement has an explicit technical realization;
- DESIGN pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- C01-C22 all applicable technical columns are `TECHNICAL_CLOSED`;
- no duplicated semantic owner or transport/persistence/UI shadow shape is introduced;
- complete producer -> carrier -> consumer paths are specified for every applicable functional obligation, including positively reused/no-change realizations;
- the final solution design explicitly maps work kind/schema/template/lifecycle context/OpenCode STAGE behavior/governed output/Workbench composition/downstream consumer wherever applicable;
- no technical obligation is represented only by POSITIVE_NO_CHANGE, baseline reuse, "existing flow" or a certification action without its complete technical realization;
- failure/recovery and negative behavior have technical owners;
- permanent verification consequences are defined;
- horizontal technical integration audit returns PASS.

## 13. Phase 3 — PLAN final implementation plan

### 13.1 Plan construction

Only after G4 may the canonical `implementation-plan.md` be finalized.

Every P7 task must provide:

- objective;
- dependencies;
- exact scope;
- explicit exclusions;
- acceptance references;
- expected semantic change surface;
- required gates;
- exact authority references;
- concern IDs covered.

### 13.2 Bidirectional coverage proof

Before PLAN can close, prove both directions:

```text
every required design obligation -> at least one P7 task
every P7 task -> one justified upstream design/requirement obligation
```

A task without upstream justification is scope creep.

A design/requirement without a task is incomplete implementation planning.

### 13.3 G5 — implementation-plan gate

G5 requires:

- all applicable concerns are `PLAN_COVERED`;
- PLAN pair audits A01-A11 are all `PASS`;
- the complete task dependency graph is executable;
- task scopes do not overlap through duplicate semantic ownership;
- the union of task scopes covers the designed change surface;
- exclusions are explicit;
- the gate union covers the verification required by the reconstructed functional/technical design and each signed task surface; existing UI/browser regression tests do not become preservation requirements merely because they already exist;
- the plan contains one valid canonical `order-tasks` block;
- no implementation worker must choose between material design alternatives.

## 13A. Phase 3A — ALIGN final vertical domain completion

ALIGN is the campaign's **mandatory final content-completion stage**. It begins only after G5 has established a complete PLAN candidate and it completes before R3 may begin.

ALIGN does not create product, technical or implementation authority. It treats the exact current R2 `requirements.md`, DESIGN `solution-design.md` and PLAN `implementation-plan.md` as untrusted inputs and proves that the same obligations are complete, semantically consistent and executable **vertically through all three layers**. R1 and current implementation/UI/tests/history may be consulted only as evidence for challenging those mappings; they do not define the desired 0.6.0 baseline.

The purpose of ALIGN is to prevent layer-local PASS results from hiding cross-layer omissions, semantic drift, delegated architecture decisions, unowned verification obligations or an implementation sequence that cannot actually execute the designed solution.

### 13A.1 Entry contract

ALIGN may start only when:

- G3, G4 and G5 are `PASS`;
- the exact current R2, DESIGN and PLAN Git blob identities are recorded in the `ALIGN-A01` baseline;
- all required PLAN source-component hashes resolve to the bytes compiled into the root PLAN;
- no unresolved campaign finding already invalidates R2, DESIGN or PLAN;
- the auditor is evaluating the package rather than repairing it in place.

A mutation to R2, DESIGN, PLAN, a permanent authority owner materially consumed by them, or a structural fitness rule that changes the meaning of the package invalidates every affected ALIGN audit and G5A until the exact corrected package is re-audited.

### 13A.2 Domain sequence

ALIGN uses the campaign's existing dependency-ordered A01-A11 domain grouping. It does not invent a second taxonomy.

| Alignment audit | Concerns | Vertical domain |
|---|---|---|
| `ALIGN-A01` | C01 + C21 | lifecycle, product model, outcome and product language |
| `ALIGN-A02` | C22 + C02 | project authority/governance and WorkItem intake |
| `ALIGN-A03` | C03 + C04 | queue, source admission and repository/document Git |
| `ALIGN-A04` | C05 + C06 | Workbench/current attention and Research/human input |
| `ALIGN-A05` | C07 + C08 | Requirements and DESIGN authority mutation |
| `ALIGN-A06` | C09 + C10 | PLAN contract and R3/readiness |
| `ALIGN-A07` | C11 + C12 | Approval, execution, checkpoints and validation |
| `ALIGN-A08` | C13 + C14 | terminal completion and governed materials |
| `ALIGN-A09` | C15 + C16 | provider integration, installation and Settings |
| `ALIGN-A10` | C17 + C18 | recovery/current attention and accessibility/focus |
| `ALIGN-A11` | C19 + C20 | component/theme system and end-to-end certification |

The sequence is mandatory. `ALIGN-A(n+1)` may not start until `ALIGN-An` is `PASS`. Reordering is permitted only when newly proven dependency evidence requires it; the dependency and revised order must be recorded here before the affected audit begins.

### 13A.3 Mandatory vertical trace

For every material atomic obligation in the domain, the audit must prove the complete chain:

```text
canonical functional authority
  -> exact R2 required behavior / state / transition / failure / recovery / acceptance
  -> exact DESIGN realization or evidence-backed positive-no-change disposition
  -> canonical semantic owner and producer -> carrier -> consumer / effect path
  -> exact PLAN IA action or evidence-backed no-code action
  -> executable dependency/order/checkpoint
  -> exact permanent proof proposition / test / sensor / gate
```

The audit must also prove the reverse direction:

```text
every DESIGN decision -> accepted functional obligation
every PLAN action -> accepted DESIGN/functional obligation
every verification mutation -> exact behavior/architecture proposition it proves
```

Shared technical solutions or implementation actions may satisfy multiple functional obligations only when every relationship is explicit and the shared owner is semantically correct. Crossover is not a defect; **implicit crossover is**.

Counts, matching IDs, hashes, green gates or presence of an SD/IA identifier are necessary structural evidence but are never sufficient semantic proof.

### 13A.3.1 Mandatory ALIGN quality standard

Every ALIGN audit applies the same non-negotiable quality bar. The purpose is not to demonstrate that artifacts are broadly consistent; it is to prove that **nothing material is omitted, compressed beyond safe traceability, delegated implicitly, or left for implementation inference**.

For each atomic obligation, the auditor records every applicable quality dimension below as `PROVEN`. `PARTIAL`, `MISSING`, `INFERRED`, `STALE` or `NOT_CHECKED` is a domain `FAIL`, not a qualified pass.

| Quality ID | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Q1 — Authority completeness | Every applicable stable ID **and normative clause without a stable ID**, P2/P3 correction, package-local consequence and unresolved/imported dependency is represented. Identifier cardinality cannot substitute for clause-level coverage. |
| Q2 — Functional explicitness | R2 itself states the complete applicable work-kind/stage/information contract: observable behavior, displayed/entered/generated/reviewed information, hierarchy, interaction, state/action/transition, blocker/failure/recovery/return/terminal semantics, acceptance, exclusions and downstream consequence. A REFERENCE/stable-ID row, concern summary or certification matrix cannot substitute for the functional description. |
| Q3 — Technical completeness | DESIGN itself resolves the exact canonical semantic owner, shared-versus-kind/stage-specific boundary, schema/template/context/prompt/provider/session consequences, document/event/state carrier, pure/effect ownership, Workbench composition, producer→carrier→consumer/data-control path, reuse/removal/replacement decision, required decomposition, downstream invariants, failure/recovery behavior, proof owner and material rejected alternatives or explicit N/A rationale. POSITIVE_NO_CHANGE/DESIGN-positive reuse/certification alone cannot substitute for this realization. |
| Q4 — Positive-no-change proof | A no-change conclusion is requirement-specific and positively proves the current canonical realization against the exact R1 evidence. “Existing behavior”, green tests, concern closure or a generic preservation statement is insufficient. |
| Q5 — Corrected-upstream retention | An obligation already corrected in P2/P3/P4-P6 remains explicitly represented downstream as an evidence-backed `NO_CODE / ALREADY_CORRECTED` disposition. It must not disappear merely because no T1 edit remains. |
| Q6 — Implementation exactness | Every technical delta has one exact IA execution owner with concrete mutation, file/module/symbol responsibility, forbidden alternatives, dependencies and completion evidence. Every IA action traces upward to accepted functional/design authority. |
| Q7 — Executability | Ordered actions and architecture-feedback checkpoints are executable from the actual signed baseline/runtime. No bootstrap, self-hosting, permission or sequencing assumption is implicit. |
| Q8 — Verification proposition closure | Every changed or positively preserved obligation identifies the **exact proposition** to prove, exact test/sensor owner, required mutation/no-mutation, and gate. Generic “add regression coverage”, “semantic review”, “tests pass” or later gap-filling is insufficient for a known proposition. |
| Q9 — Cross-domain interface closure | Every exported/imported producer, carrier, state, authority, outcome, proof or execution dependency agrees with its consuming domain and has a named owner. |
| Q10 — Minimum-change / architecture integrity | The package neither preserves a defective current shape nor forces new responsibility into a fixed file list. Architecture-preserving same-layer decomposition remains permitted exactly as higher authority allows; semantic architecture changes return upstream. |
| Q11 — Freshness and identity | Claims bind to the exact current authority/R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN bytes and current repository evidence. Any material mutation invalidates affected conclusions transitively. |
| Q12 — Zero residual inference | After R1 + R2 + DESIGN + PLAN are read, an implementation worker has **zero material product, information-design, work-kind/stage-specialization, OpenCode-integration, UI-composition, architecture, ownership, contract, sequencing or verification decision left to reconstruct or make**. Required meaning may be referenced to its canonical owner, but the package mapping may not depend on reading current implementation/tests to discover the design. |

Additional quality laws:

- **No compression loss:** a grouped row may be used only when every atomic obligation within it has the same owner, realization, execution disposition and proof. Otherwise it must be decomposed.
- **No orphaned correction:** corrected authority, deleted behavior and explicit no-code outcomes remain traceable through the matrix even when they produce no T1 mutation.
- **No proof by adjacency:** a nearby test, shared gate or broader certification row is not proof unless the exact proposition is stated and owned.
- **No “eventual closure” substitution:** a generic final regression/closeout action cannot own a proof obligation that is already known during DESIGN/PLAN.
- **No aggregate PASS override:** one failed atomic row or one failed quality dimension fails the domain.
- **No downstream progress after FAIL:** the earliest defective owner is reopened immediately; the next ALIGN domain remains blocked until the corrected package is freshly re-audited and the current domain passes.

The audit record must include a quality summary showing Q1-Q12 as `PROVEN` or the exact failed dimension/finding ID. This quality section is normative for every `ALIGN-Axx` and `ALIGN-H1` audit.

### 13A.4 Per-domain audit requirements

Each `ALIGN-Axx` audit must adversarially prove all of the following:

1. **Functional completeness** — every material authority/R1 obligation has an explicit R2 behavior, state/action/transition, failure/recovery/negative behavior, acceptance and exclusion consequence where applicable.
2. **Functional -> technical completeness** — every R2 obligation has an exact DESIGN realization, including canonical owner, affected boundaries, data/control path, persistence/transport/provider consequences and required failure/recovery semantics.
3. **Technical -> implementation completeness** — every DESIGN obligation maps to an exact PLAN action or positively proven no-code disposition; known DESIGN work must not be delegated to a generic later gap-filling action.
4. **Implementation -> authority legitimacy** — every IA action maps upward to accepted DESIGN and functional authority; no orphan action or speculative implementation is permitted.
5. **Ownership integrity** — one canonical semantic owner is preserved; no DTO, shadow model, duplicate persistence/transport/UI policy or browser-owned business policy is introduced.
6. **Executable ordering** — dependencies, internal task ordering and architecture-feedback checkpoints can actually execute on the runtime/baseline that performs the signed task; no self-hosting or bootstrap assumption is left implicit.
7. **Change-surface integrity** — task/change-surface constraints do not force duplicate responsibility, prevent authority-permitted architecture-preserving decomposition or hide an unsigned architecture decision.
8. **Verification completeness** — every functional acceptance and technical invariant has an exact proof owner and PLAN verification action where mutation is required; generic final regression closure may not substitute for a known proof obligation.
9. **Positive-no-change validity** — every no-functional/no-technical/no-code conclusion has requirement-specific positive evidence for the complete obligation and its proof owner; current implementation behavior alone is never authority.
10. **Cross-domain interfaces** — every producer/carrier/consumer or state/dependency exported to another Axx domain agrees with what that domain consumes.
11. **Minimum sufficient change** — no planned mutation exists merely because the current implementation has it, and no required correction is omitted merely to preserve an earlier file list, test or implementation shape.
12. **Zero material inference** — an implementation worker must not decide product behavior, semantic ownership, architecture, task contract, verification semantics or between material solution alternatives.

Any single failed item makes the domain audit `FAIL`.

### 13A.5 Required audit matrix and record

Every `ALIGN-Axx` record must use the exact current repository/artifact baseline and include a row for every material atomic obligation:

| Obligation | R2 functional contract | DESIGN technical realization | PLAN action/no-code | Exact proof | Cross-domain deps | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The record must then explicitly list:

- missing functional obligations;
- R2 obligations without complete technical realization;
- DESIGN decisions without upstream functional authority;
- DESIGN obligations without exact PLAN coverage;
- PLAN actions without upstream justification;
- known proof propositions without exact PLAN ownership;
- execution-order/bootstrap/checkpoint defects;
- change-surface/architecture-decomposition conflicts;
- cross-domain contradictions;
- the earliest owning lifecycle layer for every defect.

The canonical record form is:

```text
ALIGN AUDIT START: ALIGN-Axx

Domain / concerns:
Exact repository commit:
R2 blob:
DESIGN blob:
PLAN blob:
Prerequisite ALIGN audit:

Atomic vertical matrix:
<rows>

Reverse trace:
<DESIGN -> functional>
<PLAN -> DESIGN/functional>
<proof -> proposition>

Cross-domain imports/exports:
<rows>

Findings:
<IDs or NONE>

Verdict: PASS | FAIL
Failure route: R1 | R2 | DESIGN | PLAN | N/A

ALIGN AUDIT END: ALIGN-Axx
```

### 13A.6 Failure, correction and staleness law

ALIGN is an audit stage, not a repair stage.

When `ALIGN-Axx` finds a defect:

1. record the finding and identify the **earliest defective owner**;
2. return to R1, R2, DESIGN or PLAN as required by that ownership;
3. invalidate every downstream artifact/conclusion whose inputs changed;
4. keep the failed ALIGN record as historical evidence only;
5. regenerate/revalidate the affected package under the ordinary lifecycle gates;
6. restart the affected ALIGN audit on the exact new R2/DESIGN/PLAN blobs;
7. re-run every later ALIGN audit whose assumptions or evidence transitively depended on the changed material.

A later domain may not be audited against a package containing an earlier failed domain. Findings may not be accumulated while progression continues.

A correction must be made in the existing canonical owner. ALIGN must not create a shadow specification, technical design, task plan or verification model to make the audit pass.

### 13A.7 ALIGN-H1 — integrated cross-domain closure

After `ALIGN-A01` through `ALIGN-A11` all pass on one mutually compatible exact package, perform `ALIGN-H1`.

`ALIGN-H1` must prove:

- the complete create/source -> R1 -> R2 -> DESIGN -> PLAN -> R3 -> Approval -> Execution -> Complete flow is vertically coherent;
- the §4.3 final design-set matrix is complete across every applicable concern/work-kind/workflow-step/information/interaction combination, with explicit R2 functional descriptions and explicit DESIGN technical realizations rather than reference/no-change/certification shorthand;
- all valid upstream-return, stale-authority, blocker/retry/resume, provider-failure, source-change and settings-readiness paths retain the same functional -> technical -> implementation semantics;
- cross-domain producer/carrier/consumer interfaces agree;
- shared SD/IA actions have one canonical owner and all consumers are explicitly represented;
- the complete task dependency graph and every required architecture-feedback checkpoint are executable from the signed baseline;
- task concern IDs, authority refs, acceptance refs, change surfaces and gate unions cover the semantics they claim to govern;
- permanent proof ownership covers every changed and positively preserved obligation at the correct boundary;
- no package-local atom, stable requirement, DESIGN decision, IA action or proof proposition is semantically orphaned;
- no implementation worker must infer or choose a material P1-P6 decision.

`ALIGN-H1` fails if individual domains pass but their combined package is contradictory or non-executable.

### 13A.8 G5A — final vertical alignment gate

G5A is the final content-completeness gate before R3.

G5A passes only when:

- `ALIGN-A01` through `ALIGN-A11` are all `PASS` against the exact current R2/DESIGN/PLAN package;
- `ALIGN-H1` is `PASS` against those same mutually compatible bytes;
- every ALIGN finding is resolved at its owning lifecycle layer and the affected audits have been freshly rerun;
- the complete functional -> technical -> implementation -> proof trace is bidirectionally complete;
- all cross-domain dependencies and interfaces are closed;
- task ordering/checkpoint/bootstrap semantics are executable;
- no material decision remains delegated to implementation;
- no current implementation/UI/test/history behavior has been promoted into desired-state authority;
- the package is complete by semantic audit, not merely by cardinality, hash or structural fitness;
- permanent campaign fitness structurally enforces the ALIGN progression boundary before G5A may pass: exactly one current START/END record per required ALIGN audit, predecessor ordering, exact reviewed R2/DESIGN/PLAN identities, prohibition on G6 PASS before G5A PASS, and no active sibling WorkItem artifact may project a competing campaign progression/readiness checklist. The sensor must not infer semantic correctness; that remains owned by the governed ALIGN audits.

G5A `PASS` closes vertical package alignment and permits fresh R3. It does **not** authorize bounded realization, ordinary T1 execution, IA29+, or human sign-off. Under `GW-DOC-SYSTEMS-GOVERNANCE-PYRAMID-V1`, any declared IA27/IA28 bounded acceptance-evidence tranche becomes eligible only after current R3/G6 returns `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.

## 14. Phase 4 — R3 readiness and campaign closure

R3 is an adversarial review pass and treats R1/R2/DESIGN/PLAN and the ALIGN evidence as untrusted. It does not assume a separate human or worker; separation is by governed stage and evaluation posture, not personnel.

R3 may begin only after G5A is `PASS` against the exact current package. A prior R3 verdict from bytes that have not passed the current G5A stage is historical evidence only and cannot authorize sign-off.

When the current PLAN requires bounded **pre-sign-off acceptance evidence** for a design-bearing foundation, P0 ordering is strict: R3 first reviews the exact current P1-P7 package and G6 reaches `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`; only then may §17A record an explicit operator authorization bound to that exact R3-reviewed package. The grant is limited to PLAN-declared IA27/IA28 realization/evidence, stops before IA29, and exists solely for the next human evidence-acceptance/sign-off decision. If the bounded work discovers a product, architecture, contract, validation or out-of-scope defect, it returns upstream and invalidates readiness as applicable; bounded implementation itself never becomes authority.

### G6 — readiness gate

G6 passes only with `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.

R3 must specifically challenge:

- G5A freshness and whether all ALIGN-A01-A11 / ALIGN-H1 records bind to the exact package R3 is reviewing;
- concern-register completeness;
- whether any concern was closed without a START/END record;
- unresolved incoming cross-concern findings;
- P2 -> P3 -> P4/P5/P6 -> P7 traceability;
- authority/version lineage;
- minimum-change discipline;
- missing negative/failure/recovery coverage;
- implementation ambiguity;
- stale R1 evidence caused by later repository changes.

After G6 and explicit sign-off, this campaign document's status becomes `READY_FOR_IMPLEMENTATION`. Actual execution remains governed by the signed P7 tasks and normal Order execution/validation lifecycle.

## 15. Horizontal integration audits

Individual concern closure is necessary but insufficient.

Three horizontal audits are mandatory.

### H1 — post-R1 factual integration

Before G2:

- prove adjacent concern assumptions agree;
- prove all cross-concern findings have owners;
- identify facts whose evidence changed during later concern reviews;
- revalidate early concerns where necessary.

### H2 — post-R2 functional integration

Before G3, walk the complete product:

```text
install/readiness
-> queue
-> create/source
-> R1
-> R2
-> DESIGN
-> PLAN
-> R3
-> Approval
-> Execution
-> Complete
```

Also walk every valid upstream return, stale-authority path, blocker/retry/resume path, source-change path, provider failure path and settings-readiness recovery.

### H3 — post-DESIGN technical integration

Before G4:

- one owner per semantic concept;
- complete producer/carrier/consumer agreement;
- no DTO/shadow representation;
- no browser-owned business policy;
- no duplicate persistence/transport authority;
- no effect leakage into functional core;
- complete failure/recovery ownership;
- verification at the correct boundary.

## 16. Campaign tracking rules

### 16.1 Status updates

Update this plan after:

- each concern START;
- each concern END;
- each cross-concern finding creation/disposition;
- each concern reaching functional closure;
- each concern reaching technical closure;
- each concern gaining final plan coverage;
- each ALIGN-A01-A11 START/END and verdict;
- ALIGN-H1 START/END and verdict;
- each phase gate verdict, including G5A;
- any foundational reopening.

Status updates record evidence and references; they do not restate normative design.

### 16.2 Evidence baseline

Each START/END record identifies the exact repository commit used for its evidence.

If a later authority/code change materially affects already-closed evidence, the concern moves to `REVALIDATION`. It cannot silently remain closed.

If a later mutation materially affects an R2/DESIGN/PLAN obligation or proof consumed by a passed ALIGN audit, the affected ALIGN audit and every transitively dependent later ALIGN audit return to `REVALIDATION`; G5A and G6 cannot remain PASS on stale bytes.

### 16.3 No skipped states

A concern cannot jump:

- `NOT_STARTED -> FACTS_CLOSED` without START/END records;
- R1 -> `FUNCTIONAL_CLOSED` without factual closure;
- R2 -> `TECHNICAL_CLOSED` without functional closure;
- DESIGN -> `PLAN_COVERED` without technical closure where technical design applies;
- PLAN/G5 -> R3 without G5A PASS;
- ALIGN-A(n) -> ALIGN-A(n+1) without the prior ALIGN audit PASS;
- ALIGN-A11 -> ALIGN-H1 without ALIGN-A01-A11 all PASS;
- any phase -> `CLOSED` while incoming findings remain unresolved.

## 17A. Bounded pre-sign-off realization authorization

**Current bounded authorization: NONE.**

**Immediate operator-directed PR remediation priority — P0.R (UI-01–UI-06).** On 2026-11-26 the operator rejected the actual rendered candidate `53b1c0e543d8a44f8d77da3b51f11c3b0d802978` (PR #83, Actions 45735014352), explicitly directed immediate integrated correction of the six specific defects below and required one consolidated reference comparison only after all corrections are integrated. The priority is an operator direction for the PR candidate and a binding rejection/proof input under the existing C19-F09/C20-F11 and HUX acceptance law, not a new P7 task contract, sign-off, gate PASS or HUX disposition. The existing T1 and exact reviewed P3/P4/DESIGN/PLAN package remain unchanged. ## 19. Completion definition

The 0.6.0 design campaign is complete only when all of the following are true:

1. G0-G5, G5A and G6 are PASS.
2. Every registered concern is `CLOSED` or explicitly marked not applicable with evidence.
3. No concern's **Overall** current state is `IN_REVIEW`, `BLOCKED`, `REVALIDATION`, `GAP_FOUND`, or carrying unresolved incoming findings; an R1 phase cell may remain `GAP_FOUND` as the closed factual record of a gap that was subsequently disposed.
4. Every R1 finding has a final disposition.
5. Every R2 requirement has one canonical owner and acceptance criteria.
6. Every applicable work-kind/workflow-step/information/interaction obligation has a complete explicit functional description in R2 and a complete explicit technical realization in DESIGN under §4.3; references/no-change/certification rows alone are insufficient.
7. Every functional obligation has a complete technical realization.
8. Every technical/design obligation has exact P7 implementation or evidence-backed no-code coverage.
9. Every P7 action has upstream functional/design justification.
10. Every mandatory pair audit for R1, R2, DESIGN and PLAN is `PASS`.
11. ALIGN-A01 through ALIGN-A11 are `PASS` against the exact current R2/DESIGN/PLAN package.
12. ALIGN-H1 is `PASS` and proves integrated cross-domain executability and proof ownership.
13. The complete functional -> technical -> implementation -> proof trace is bidirectionally closed with no material inference delegated to implementation.
14. The final horizontal audits pass.
15. R3, run only after G5A PASS and before any bounded acceptance-evidence realization, returns `READY_FOR_SIGNOFF`.
16. When the current PLAN requires the §17A IA27/IA28 bounded evidence tranche, it is explicitly authorized only after G6 PASS, terminates before IA29, and its evidence is reviewed before explicit human sign-off.

The campaign then hands one exact, traceable package to explicit sign-off. It does not authorize implementation by status assertion alone.

## 20. Derived execution-state view

No current campaign status is maintained in this section.

Consumers derive current state from §7 and §18 only. In particular:

- the active concern is the unique §7 concern whose applicable phase is `IN_REVIEW`, unless a §7 `BLOCKED`/`REVALIDATION` state explicitly stops progression;
- the current campaign phase is the first gate in §18 that is not `PASS`; once all gates pass, an active §17A grant denotes the bounded pre-sign-off evidence activity until that grant terminates;
- no prose summary, handover, prior model output, START/END record or PR status may override those registers.

This section intentionally contains no manually maintained status projection.

## 21. Historical evidence location

Superseded pair/ALIGN audits, prior R3/sign-off records, RZ1-RZ12 grant bodies and earlier implementation-audit narratives remain durable in Git history, PR verification runs/artifacts and ordinary repository provenance rather than in this live control surface. They are evidence only and cannot override §7, §8.4, §13A, §17A or §18.
